# AUTOPILOT: the work queue that runs without the user (created 2026-11-05)

Goal: the user can walk away for hours or days. Nothing below needs the user. Every question that comes up is written to `docs/agent/ASK_LATER.md`
(one line, with what was assumed) and work continues with the assumption. Never wait for an answer.

## MAIN TARGET (set by the user 2026-11-05): the whole SHAPE of the distribution must match reality, not only ranges and means
Everything else in this file is judged by whether it moves these numbers. When two items compete for the GPU, the one that moves a shape target goes first. Rule 4 below gets one more condition: **an item may not make any shape number worse** (beyond its noise), and a change that improves a shape number with the usual skill rule holding is kept even if the mean skill15 gain is below 0.001.

Where the system stands (measured, p2f, 2024-25, ERA5 / balloons, from `models/generator_80_p2f_*_extras_{coverage,pit}.csv`, 2026-11-05):
- Interior shape is decent: coverage at the 50 / 80 / 90 / 99 % levels is off by on average 0.015 / 0.015 / 0.015 / 0.003 on ERA5 and 0.016 / 0.015 / 0.013 / 0.004 on balloons (the middle of the distribution is slightly too wide in the low-level T, RH and surface cells, slightly too narrow in T 5-12 km and in v aloft).
- Probability-integral-transform (PIT) histograms, as total-variation distance from flat (0 is perfect): mean 0.033 on ERA5, 0.050 on balloons; worst cells ERA5 T 5-12 km 0.063, T 0-5 km 0.052, t2m 0.048; balloons RH 5-12 km 0.131, RH 12-18 km 0.086, t2m 0.074, sp 0.069.
- NOT yet measured: skewness and kurtosis of the predicted vs real distributions, shape by regime (season, hour, ENSO, QBO), the joint shape across heights and variables (vertical correlation, cross-variable closure), and the tails beyond the 99th percentile. The existing joint checks say: whole-profile 99 % band holds 0.903 of RH profiles (target 0.95), the variogram score is only compared between models.

### Shape targets (the Shape Fidelity Index, SFI; each a number measured by `scripts/diagnose.py`, IL-43/44)
| ID | Target | Now | Goal |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | Marginal PIT distance from flat, mean over cells (ERA5 / balloons) | 0.033 / 0.050 | <= 0.020 / <= 0.030; worst cell <= 0.05 |
| S2 | Coverage error at the 10 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 80 / 90 / 95 / 99 % levels, mean abs | 0.015 at 50-90 | <= 0.008 at every level |
| S3 | Skewness and excess kurtosis of the standardized outcome vs of the standardized sample, per cell (difference) | unmeasured | <= 0.15 and <= 0.4 |
| S4 | Conditional shape: PIT distance by season x hour x ENSO x QBO regime, worst regime | unmeasured | <= 0.06 |
| S5 | Joint shape: vertical correlation matrix error per variable (M6), multivariate rank histogram flatness, whole-profile 99 % coverage (T, RH) | RH 0.903 | T >= 0.95, RH >= 0.95 |
| S6 | Tail shape: exceedance frequency at the 0.1 / 1 / 99 / 99.9 % climatological quantiles vs nominal, shear distribution KS (M2, M3, M7) | 99.9th shear 6-20 % low | within 20 % of nominal rate; shear KS <= 0.03 |
| S7 | Group-distribution distance: Wasserstein-1 between the unknown-driver samples and the real 2024-25 outcomes of the same month x hour-bin group, divided by the climate spread | unmeasured | <= 0.05 |
| S8 | Cross-variable shape: relation of the samples between variables (u-v direction spread, T-RH correlation, lapse rate vs RH) vs the same in ERA5 and balloons (M8) | unmeasured | correlation differences <= 0.05 |

### Queue items aimed straight at shape (group SH; they run right after IL-8 and before everything else that does not target shape)
| ID | Work | Why it should help shape | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| IL-43 | `metrics_ext.py` + `scripts/diagnose.py` with S1-S8 and M1-M18 (as listed in the metrics section) | cannot improve what is not measured | first batch done (PIT, coverage levels, skew/kurtosis, tail rates, event-conditional coverage, bias by month, spread-skill, vertical correlation, shear distance, drift proxy, direction, Brier; 6 tests); still to add: S7 group distance, Hersbach decomposition, regime splits (M9), memorisation (M15), driver response (M16), balloon version |
| IL-44 | Run it on p2f (ERA5, balloons), publish the table, add one backlog row per weakest metric | focuses the work | queued: `scripts/ap_sh_chain.sh` runs it after group S (ERA5 only; log data/ap_sh.log, output models/generator_80_p2f_base_diag2.csv) |
| IL-46 | **Full PIT recalibration**: per variable x band, an isotonic fit of the PIT cumulative distribution on 2021-23, applied as a rank-preserving map over ALL probability levels (not only the range ends); optionally per season | repairs the interior shape directly (S1, S2); the present map mainly fixes the 99 % ends | todo |
| IL-47 | **Shape-aware training loss**: variogram score over height pairs and a pinball loss on a grid of 9 quantile levels added to the CRPS terms, small weights, off by default | the loss decides the shape; CRPS with 4 samples is a weak signal for skew and kurtosis | todo |
| IL-48 | **Larger loss sample m = 8** (the training loss uses 4 samples per row; more samples give a less noisy CRPS and energy score) | cleaner gradient for the distribution shape; costs about 2x time | todo |
| IL-49 | **Distribution-matching auxiliary loss** (sliced Wasserstein or maximum mean discrepancy between the sample batch and the target batch per height) | targets marginal shape without needing a pointwise truth | todo |
| IL-50 | **Skew-capable noise**: replace the Gaussian noise by a mixture (Gaussian plus a skewed or heavy-tailed component) as network input | the network's output shape inherits the noise shape; skewed variables (RH, wind speed, shear) may need it | todo |
| IL-51 | **Regime-wise PIT recalibration**: separate PIT maps for ENSO phase and QBO phase (only if S4 shows regime effects) | conditional shape | todo (after IL-44) |
| IL-52 | **Mixture-density head for the bounded variables** (RH near 0 and 100, wind speed >= 0): a point mass plus continuous part | the clipped RH ends are a shape feature the Gaussian-noise network handles crudely | todo |
| IL-53 | **Copula check and repair for the vertical structure**: compare the sample copula with the ERA5 copula (rank correlation between heights); if different, a rank-reordering step (Schaake-shuffle style) using real ERA5 profiles as dependence templates | keeps marginals and puts the real joint shape back | todo |

## How a session wakes up and continues (read this first after any pause, summary or restart)
1. Read this file; BLOCK 0 (immediate fixes, F-1 ... F-17) comes first: work through it in order before any other row. Then find the first row below with state `todo` or `running`. Check `data/` logs for its result files.
2. If the item is `running`: look at its log; if finished, evaluate it (rule 4) and set it `done`/`discarded`/`blocked`; if the process is dead, relaunch the script (they resume).
3. Launch the next `todo` item. GPU jobs: one at a time. While a GPU job runs, do CPU-side prep for the next one (code, tests, docs).
4. Verdict rule for every item (shape rule from the MAIN TARGET section applies on top: no shape number may get worse, a shape gain is a gain): run `scripts/compare_headline.py` on ERA5 and on balloons against the adopted model (p2f). **Keep** if the phase pass rule holds on both (mean skill15 not lower, no band worse with non-overlapping intervals, mean cov99 error not worse by more than 0.001) AND there is a real gain (mean skill15 up by more than 0.001, or mean cov99 error down by more than 0.0005 without a band getting worse). Otherwise **discard** (flag stays off; code stays; one `salvage` look: which bands gained, can a restricted version be queued once).
4b. **Shape half of the verdict (2026-11-05):** also run `scripts/shape_verdict.sh VAR` (diagnose + `compare_shape.py`, tolerance = seed spread of p2f from IL-92). A candidate is kept only if neither the headline rule nor the shape rule fails; a shape gain beyond the seed spread is a gain even when skill ties. Details: `docs/results/APPLIED_STATUS.md`.
5. Log every item as a row in `docs/plans/PLAN.md` (improvement-loop queue table plus a result line), commit locally (small commits, NO Claude attribution, never `data/` or `models/`). **Push policy (user 2026-11-05): do NOT push to GitHub until the user approves or at the very end of all the work.**
5b. **Stage 6 (user 2026-11-05):** after BLOCK 0, the improvement loop, the final model (every modification tested) and group C (coherence, user 2026-11-06) comes stage 6 = tools for the flight simulator (USER 2026-11-06: start it yourself once everything before it is complete, no permission needed; tools only, no model changes; the push policy is unchanged), formerly only after the user approves. Plan file `C:\Users\Senai\Downloads\tempest_arknet_flight_sim_expansion_blueprint.md`: do NOT read it before the final model is built; then adjust and improve it. Anything that needs the user's permission is sidelined (ASK_LATER) and asked at the very end; do not stop to ask during the improvement loop.
5c. **GPU hygiene (found 2026-11-05):** never run pytest or scripts that touch CUDA while a GPU job runs (two crashes with 'CUDA out of memory' at launch); CPU-only checks use `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""` and deselect `tests/test_flags_off.py` (its golden numbers are for the GPU).
6. Machine rules: 12 CPUs (`source scripts/cap_cpu.sh`), one GPU job, never edit a running script, never run pytest while a scoring job runs, 2026 Jan-Jun is only touched in the final test (E4).
7. NO stop rule on failures (user decision 2026-11-05): a string of discarded items never ends the loop. Keep taking the next item. Group E starts only when the queue AND the idea backlog (below) hold no `todo` item that can run without the user; items waiting on user data stay `blocked-on-data` and are re-checked each tick.
7b. **Idea intake (standing rule):** every time a possible improvement shows up (a limit found while building, a pattern in a result, a bias that a fix leaves open, a new source, a salvageable part of a discarded item), append it at once to the idea backlog below as a new `IL-n` row (hypothesis, why it might work, how to test, state `todo`). Do this when the idea appears, not later. The backlog is part of the queue.
8. Safety net: a recurring wake-up prompt ("Autopilot tick") re-enters this file every ~25 minutes while the session lives. It is session-only; if the session ended, a new session reads this file and continues.

## Usefulness goals (added 2026-11-05 after the assessment: 7/10 as a Monte Carlo source, 9 vs standard atmosphere, 6 vs plain climatology)
The model is accurate to about the climatology level and well calibrated; what makes it valuable is joint structure. These goals raise the usefulness for Monte Carlo
work and beyond, each with a measurable target. They are queue items (group U); same verdict and logging rules; none needs the user (the first one removes the need for the user's simulator).

| ID | Goal | Why it helps | Test / success criterion | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## Diagnostic metrics (added 2026-11-05): 18 new measurements, all computable offline from the scorer's samples and truth (no simulator)
Why: today the headline table (skill15, cov99, MAE) says whether a change helps, not WHERE the system is weak. These metrics locate weaknesses and each one can turn into a backlog idea.
Implementation: `tempest/scoring/metrics_ext.py` (one function per metric, unit-tested on synthetic data) and `scripts/diagnose.py MODEL CALIB DATA` which runs them for ERA5 and balloons 2024-25 (flags such as `--shift`, `--trust` pass through) and writes `<stem>_diag2.csv` plus a ranked "weakest ten" list.
Rule: from IL-44 on, every experiment is also run through `diagnose.py`; the weakest ten metrics of the adopted model become new backlog rows (idea intake rule 7b).

| # | Metric | What it measures | Weakness it can expose | Exists today? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | PIT calibration error: area between the PIT histogram and uniform, and its slope (bias) and U-shape (spread) parts, per variable x band x season | whether probabilities are right everywhere, not only at 99 % | spread too narrow in one season; systematic bias vs spread problems told apart | histograms only |
| M2 | Tail-weighted CRPS (weights on the upper and lower 5 % of the climate distribution) and exceedance frequencies at the 0.1, 1, 5, 95, 99, 99.9 % climatological quantiles vs their nominal rates | accuracy of the tails, where Monte Carlo risk lives | tails too light or too heavy at specific quantiles | partly (cov99 only) |
| M3 | Event-conditional coverage: among the 1 % most extreme truth cases (shear, wind speed, low and high T, high RH), the share inside the 99 % band and the mean PIT | the blind spot of unconditional coverage: a model can cover 99 % overall and miss the extreme cases | extremes systematically outside the range | no |
| M4 | Bias and median error (MAE, RMSE) by band x month x launch-hour bin, and the slope of bias against year over 2021-25 | where the cold bias sits and whether it drifts | missing warming trend (IL-19), seasonal or diurnal bias (IL-20) | bias per band only |
| M5 | Spread-skill ratio per band x season x hour (ratio of ensemble spread to median error) | under or over dispersion by regime | narrow summer T 5-12 km | training diagnostic only |
| M6 | Vertical correlation error: Frobenius distance between the sampled and the true correlation matrix of each variable's profile (44 x 44), and of the neighbour-height correlation vs lag | does the profile have the right vertical coherence | too smooth or too jagged profiles | variogram score only |
| M7 | Shear distribution distance: KS and Wasserstein distance of neighbour shear and of 1, 3 and 5 km layer shear per band, plus exceedance frequency of fixed thresholds (10, 15, 20 m/s per km) | the whole shear distribution, not two percentiles | 99.9th gap, wrong mid-range frequency | percentiles only |
| M8 | Cross-variable consistency: thermal-wind closure (vertical wind shear vs horizontal temperature gradient from coupled neighbours), hypsometric closure error, share of supersaturated and super-adiabatic samples, correlation of RH with lapse rate | physical coupling between variables | incoherent atmospheres | supersaturation and lapse shares |
| M9 | Calibration by regime: coverage and skill split by ENSO phase (ONI < -0.5, neutral, > 0.5), QBO phase (westerly, easterly), season and wind regime (monsoon-flow vs westerly); report the largest deviation | whether calibration depends on state | works in neutral, fails in El Nino | no |
| M10 | Hersbach CRPS decomposition per band: reliability, resolution, uncertainty | tells whether the weakness is bad probabilities (reliability) or lack of information (resolution) | focuses effort: reliability can be fixed by calibration, resolution cannot | no |
| M11 | Brier skill scores for decision events vs climatology: shear above 15 and 20 m/s per km, wind above 25 m/s in the 8-12 km layer, RH 2 m above 90 %, T 2 m below 0 C, gust above 20 m/s; with reliability curves | usefulness for go/no-go style questions | good CRPS but poor event probabilities | no |
| M12 | Drift proxy without a simulator: horizontal displacement of a tracer rising at 10 m/s through the sampled wind profile, at 3, 10 and 18 km; distribution (mean, spread, 95/99th) vs the same from truth profiles | the simplest downstream quantity, before IL-31's ascent model | wrong drift scatter although columns look fine | no |
| M13 | Directional scores: circular CRPS and mean angular error of wind direction, and speed CRPS, per band | direction errors that u and v scores hide | direction too random above 12 km | partly in extras |
| M14 | Seed and member stability: repeat the sampler with 20 seeds; spread of the mean, a quantile and a tail frequency across seeds, vs the 4-member and the 8-member model | how much of a result is Monte Carlo noise of the generator itself | noisy tails that make comparisons unreliable | no |
| M15 | Memorisation and novelty: distance of each sample to its nearest training profile vs the distance of truth to its nearest training profile; share of samples (and of truth) outside the training min-max per column | whether samples are copies or genuinely new, and whether the model extrapolates | copying (too small), or inventing impossible profiles (too large) | no |
| M16 | Driver response: skill with known drivers minus skill with unknown drivers (the driver gain), and the response of the median to a +-1 sigma ONI or QBO change against the sign known from climate science | whether the ENSO and QBO inputs do real work | drivers ignored or wrong-signed | known vs unknown skill only |
| M17 | Temporal and spatial coherence (needs IL-35, IL-36): correlation of consecutive hours vs lag, and correlation vs distance across the 377 grid points, sampled vs ERA5 | whether sequences and downrange fields are realistic | decorrelated hours, too-small or too-large spatial scale | no |
| M18 | Monte Carlo efficiency: standard error of a 1 % and a 0.1 % tail probability at N = 200, 1,000, 10,000 draws (bootstrap), and effective sample size under conditioning (IL-33) or weights (IL-34) | how many runs a user needs | wasteful tails | no |

## Sidelined (need the flight simulator, its owner, or outside tools; stay out of the queue until the user brings them)
- C3-C6 integration of the readers into `flight_sim` and the one-dimension LHS agreement with the EDITH owner (the readers themselves stay as built and tested).
- IL-39 parts that name OpenRocket or RASAero layouts and reference readers of those programs: only the generic CSV, NetCDF and JSON writers with a numpy reader stay in the queue.
- IL-42 other launch sites (needs new data and training; feasibility note only).
- The user's own landing-scatter test with a real vehicle file (IL-31 replaces it with a generic in-repo ascent model).
- IL-192 (2026-11-10, idea only, nothing downloaded): use the NREL/A2e SOWFA large-eddy simulation of a diurnal cycle at the SWiFT site (Lubbock, 2013-11-08 12:00 UTC to 2013-11-09 12:00 UTC, mesoscale-coupled) as a structure reference for the 0-200 m layer. Question: how do sigma_u, sigma_w, L_u, L_w and the night-jet shear change with stability (day, stable night, morning transition) against the neutral-only Dryden recipe in `tempest_weather/flight/dryden.py`? Simulation, not observation: only to choose the form; set and test values on the WFIP1 sodars (`data/raw/a2e/`) and the SWiFT tower. One simulated day at one site, so no tuning to it. Needs a look at the dataset's variables and size first.

## SHEAR TRACK (user 2026-11-05: keep looking for solutions for wind shear; runs as a standing priority next to the shape target)
State of shear (p2f, 2024-25, ERA5, m/s per km, 99th / 99.9th percentile of neighbour shear; truth in brackets): 1-5 km 18.3 / 24.0 (17.9 / 25.7), 5-12 km 20.4 / 27.1 (20.7 / 30.3), above 12 km 25.5 / 32.5 (25.1 / 34.8), 0-1 km 55.3 / 77.8 (54.4 / 82.3). So the 99th is right and the 99.9th is 5-11 % low. The 2021-23 targets differ from 2024-25 (1-5 km 99.9th: 29.2 vs 25.7), so the tail is partly a moving target: judge on both periods.
What was tried: step CRPS on neighbour steps (kept, weight 0.5); multi-stride step CRPS (rejected, weaker); shear-tail threshold-weighted CRPS (kept at 1.0, dose response not saturated: 0.5 -> 1.0 moved the 1-5 km 99.9th from 23.4 to 24.0); Earth-GRAM-style patches (IL-5, IL-12: help the 99.9th, push the 99th up, no scorer effect).

## Pitfall checks (user 2026-11-05): quick checks for the classic beginner and expert mistakes, run after every item that trains, fits or changes a file, and at the end of every phase
Automated: `PYTHONPATH=. python scripts/pitfall_check.py MODEL CALIB DATA --aux <fitted tables> [--skip-model]` (about a minute, GPU only for a few hundred samples). It must print no FAIL; every WARN gets a line in the PLAN row of the item. First run on p2f (2026-11-05): 11 pass, 0 fail, 3 warn (calibration window = early-stopping window, see IL-76).
| # | Pitfall | Check | Automated |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Training, validation, calibration and test data overlap (the user's example) | train years < validation years; calibration/fit windows end before the test years; warn when a fit window overlaps the early-stopping validation window | yes (1a, 1b, 1c) |
| 2 | Peeking at the final test (2026 Jan-Jun) | no fitted file ends in 2026; no log of a development run mentions 2026 | yes (2) |
| 3 | Stale or mismatched files | every fitted table carries the hash of this exact model; results are newer than the model | yes (3, 11) |
| 4 | Garbage outputs (NaN, impossible values, collapsed samples) | finite, RH 0-100, T and wind in physical range, no constant columns, samples distinct | yes (4, 4b) |
| 5 | Not reproducible, or seed luck | same seed gives identical samples, other seed differs; a result inside the seed-noise band (0.001-0.002 in skill15) is not a gain | yes (5) + verdict rule |
| 6 | Model ignores its inputs, or the target leaks in | shuffling the date and driver inputs must make the CRPS worse | yes (6) |
| 7 | Too good to be true | mean skill15 above 0.15 is treated as a leak until explained | yes (7) |
| 8 | Coverage bought with width | 99 % range not wider than the climate range | yes (8) |
| 9 | Normalisation or statistics computed with test data | stored means agree with the training years rather than all years | yes (9) |
| 10 | Repository hygiene | no data/models/keys tracked, no file > 5 MB, no attribution lines | yes (10) |
| 12 | Overfitting | validation loss / training loss from the stored history < 1.35 | yes (12) |
| 13 | Comparing against a weak or different baseline | every comparison uses the same rows, seed, m and code as the adopted model (scripts/compare_headline.py); the climatology baseline is the recent-15-years one | by procedure |
| 14 | Garden of forking paths: many variants, one wins by luck | with n variants in a group the gain needed to adopt one is 0.001 + 0.0005 x (n - 1) in mean skill15, and it must hold on both ERA5 and balloons | by procedure (verdict rule) |
| 15 | Tuning on the test years | choices (bands, margins, thresholds, weights) are made on 2021-23 only; if a choice is made after seeing 2024-25, the item is labelled "chosen on test" and cannot be adopted | by procedure |
| 16 | Units and ordering | column order u, v, T, RH, t2m, sp, rh2m and the 44 heights are asserted in the unit tests; surface height in metres vs geopotential was a real bug (IL-73) | tests + review |
| 17 | Averages hide the failure | every table reports the worst cell next to the mean | by procedure |
| 18 | Target or truth drifts (inhomogeneous data) | per-year bias table (`scripts/bias_by_year.py`, `scripts/era5_vs_balloon_by_year.py`) is rerun when a band looks off | by procedure |
| 19 | Trusting a metric that does not test the claim | the tail metric that compared the truth with the nominal rate instead of the model's prediction was found and fixed (IL-43); each new metric gets a synthetic test where the answer is known | tests |
| 20 | Silent fallbacks | a mode that falls back (forecast modes) sets a flag in the output; tests assert the flag | tests |
| 21 | Misaligned arrays (inputs and truth subsampled with different random indices) | draw ONE index and apply it to every array; assert an expected relationship (here: forecast error grows with lead time, the forecast beats climatology at day 1) before reading any score. Found in IL-73: the first mode F evaluation showed the forecast worse than climatology | assertion in `il6_modeF.py` |
| 22 | Mixed-unit averages (m/s, K and % averaged into one number) | always report per variable; the mean over cells is dominated by RH (errors of 10-20 %) | by procedure |
Extra check after every phase: `pytest` (nothing else running), `git status` clean apart from data/models, the PLAN row exists with numbers and intervals.

## Group R: adjustable region and dates (user 2026-11-05: check that the system can give data for any place in North America and any future date; findings in `docs/plans/REGION_PLAN.md`)
Findings: trained and served area = pilot box only (other places are refused clearly); future dates work to 2100 with flags beyond 5 years; hours limited to 06-21 solar.

## Group C: coherence add-on (user 2026-11-06: after group E, before stage 6)
Sampling-time only: no retrain, the final model and its E4 numbers stay as they are. Fit and check on years up to 2025 only (2026 stays untouched).
| ID | Step | How |
|---|---|---|
| C1 DONE 02:55 (see below) | IL-35 hourly coherence (T-3 h ... T+6 h) | fit correlation vs lag per height x variable x season on ERA5 hourly; tie draws through `tempest_weather/coupling` (GaussianCopula); check vs ERA5 lags and balloon 00Z/12Z pairs |
| C1 RESULT | `scripts/c1_fit_lagcorr.py` (ERA5 anomalies at 8 points, fit 2005-2022, lag 0-12 h, per season and column): lag-1 / 3 / 6 / 9 correlation u 0.99 / 0.94 / 0.86 / 0.79, T 0.99 / 0.96 / 0.91 / 0.86, RH 0.98 / 0.90 / 0.79 / 0.71; out of sample (2023-25) mean |difference| 0.001-0.022 (worst column 0.07-0.09). Balloon 00Z/12Z pairs are less coherent than ERA5 at 12 h (mean 0.066 lower, worst v 12-18 km 0.163): a nugget `kappa` (balloon / ERA5, 0.75-0.99, mean 0.90) per variable x band is stored with the table and applied (R = kappa R0 + (1-kappa) I). `tempest_weather/coherence.py` (Gaussian copula over time; z_i = sum L_ik e_k with e_k the normal scores of a random whole sample of hour k) + `api.atmosphere_sequence(lat, lon, when, offsets, n)`; export `--coherence`. Check (`scripts/c1_check.py`, 6 dates x 2 sites, offsets -3..6 h): lag correlation vs kappa x ERA5 mean |difference| 0.008, worst 0.024 (goal 0.05); each hour keeps its marginal (sd within 3.3 %); vertical correlation change 0.04 (sampling noise of a correlation at n = 800 is about 0.035). Limit: offsets within 12 h of each other; hours must be inside 06-21 solar | |
| C2 RESULT | DONE 03:25: `scripts/c2_fit_distcorr.py` (ERA5, 45 points, 3-hourly 2005-2022, distance bins 0 / 24 / 28 / 37 / 48 / 55 / 59 km): correlation 0.99-0.98 at 24 km, 0.96-0.94 at 59 km (ERA5 is smooth at 0.25 degrees; the real atmosphere is rougher, so the time nugget kappa is applied as an assumption); out-of-sample 2023-25 mean |difference| 0.002-0.003, max 0.02. `api.atmosphere_downrange(lat, lon, when, bearing, distances_km, n)` + `coherence.sequence(..., rho_x=km)`; export `--coherence-dist`. Check (`scripts/c2_check.py`, 6 dates x 4 bearings, 0 / 30 / 60 km): mean |difference| to kappa x ERA5 0.005, worst 0.013 (goal 0.05); marginal sd kept within 2.8 %. Not checked: ASOS station pairs (no two ASOS stations lie within 60 km in the box; MAF-TCS are 505 km apart), so the small-scale roughness rests on the kappa assumption | |
| C2 | IL-36 downrange coherence (0-60 km) | fit correlation vs distance on the ERA5 grid; same copula tie; check vs ERA5 within 0.05 and vs ASOS station pairs at the surface |
| C3 RESULT | DONE 03:50: `scripts/c3_regime.py` (regime = tercile of the 0-3 km shear, edges 6.75 / 12.43 m/s): distance correlation barely depends on the regime (largest mean |difference| to the all-days table 0.015, so ONE distance table); the hourly lag correlation does at long lags (v 12 h calm 0.639 vs windy 0.690; mean |difference| to the all-days table 0.066 for v calm 12 h, u windy 12 h 0.049, others 0.004-0.037) -> per-regime LAG tables (3 regimes x 4 seasons x 13 lags x 176 columns, `models/coherence_regimes.npz`), applied per trajectory by the shear tercile of its first sample (`coherence.sequence(..., rho_regime, regime_of)`, export `--coherence-regimes`). Check (`scripts/c3_check.py`, offsets -6 / 0 / +6 h): 12 h correlation inside each regime vs kappa x regime table mean |difference| 0.034, worst 0.097 (about 1,000 trajectories per regime, sampling noise about 0.02; the regime of a trajectory is judged by the sample that drove it, not its mapped output, which blurs the check). A learned multi-point generator is NOT needed (the copula meets 0.05 on average), so the user question about retraining does not arise | |
| C3 | Regime check | refit C1/C2 for calm / middle / windy days (0-3 km shear terciles). Lengths close: keep one fit. Far apart: fit per regime. Learned multi-point generator only if the per-regime copula still misses the 0.05 check (ask the user first: it retrains the model) |

## Rules for what to do when a step fails
- A script fails: read its log, fix the cause once, rerun. Second failure: mark the item `blocked`, add a line to ASK_LATER, take the next item.
- Out of memory or process killed: rerun the same script (all stages skip finished outputs).
- Unclear design choice: pick the simplest option, write the assumption in the PLAN row and in ASK_LATER, continue.

## Flight-sim trial findings, 2026-11-08 (`flight_sim_trial/FINDINGS.md`, deletable folder, untracked)
| IL | Finding | Next |
|---|---|---|
| IL-144 | **(narrowed by GW-1: against ASOS at TCS and MAF 2024-25 the climate-mode spread is calibrated, fitted scale 1.0; the narrow spread was specific to balloons / late June 00 UTC)** Wind spread too narrow vs balloons (Santa Teresa NM, late June 00 UTC, n=236): real u inside the tool's p5-p95 only 80-86 % (ideal 90), tool sd 15-25 % below the balloons' (u 0-1 km 4.46 vs 5.56 m/s), real speed median 1.0-1.5 m/s above the tool's below 3 km. Understates landing-drift spread | wind spread calibration 0-12 km (check with the spread/PIT recalibration, 3 seeds); repeat in winter and at a second station |
| IL-145 | **Pad elevation**: the tool's lookup gives 1493.5 m for a 1400.6 m pad; unpinned, pad pressure is 0.87 kPa low (apogee +5 m for the trial rocket). The simulator never calls `TempestAtmosphere.anchor`, so the mismatch is silent | Section 2C `elevation.py`; user-given elevation always wins; reader warns when asked below its lowest node by more than 10 m |
| IL-146 | Upper-air T coverage 85-87 % at 6-18 km (bias -0.8 K at 12-18 km), balloons 2-4 %RH moister at 3-12 km | minor; fold into the next calibration pass |
Result summary: the plain standard day is outside the whole Tempest apogee distribution (0th percentile; pad 28 K too cold, 10 % too dense); Tempest median apogee +4.4 % (afternoon), +3.6 % (morning), +4.1 % (light rocket); a scaled-standard setup recovers the afternoon within 9 m but misses the morning inversion by 55 m; real balloons fall inside the tool's T range 85-94 % (ideal 90) and the superadiabatic near-surface lapse rate matches (25.0 vs 25.3 K/km).

## Assumption checks V1-V8 (user 2026-11-08, in the to-do list before L16)
V1 surface climate vs ASOS by month x hour (includes the training target), V2 low-level wind profile shape 10-200 m vs HRRR 80 m / IGRA / ERA5, V3 balloon scores seen vs unseen stations (IL-114), V4 memorisation / novelty (M15), V5 which bands fall below the Gaussian baseline, V6 density and pad pressure vs balloons and ASOS, V7 weather(percentile) consistency, V8 independence of the apogee validation flights. Why these: the balloon check changed the reading of the shear rows; each of these tests an assumption that so far was taken from a single reference (ERA5, a headline number, three flights).
- 2026-11-09 failure ledger: G1 localisation (neutral/negative), G4 Murphy-Koop (no measurable gain) -> 2 of 3 toward the next sideline idea. W4 Weibull-space head dropped before building (premise check `scripts/w4_premise_check.py`: the calm gap is already in ERA5, calm 1.2 % vs ASOS 6.1 %, and the tool's k 2.05 is LOWER than ERA5 2.34 and ASOS 2.23, so it is not too Gaussian) = 3 of 3, new idea IL-165 below.
- IL-165 (learns from W4: the 10 m shape gap is ERA5's smoothness, not the network; W5: a station quantile map fixes it per site, KS 0.186 -> 0.086; GW-G / W10: pad night factors 0.5-0.8; M5c: a sitecal fitted on ERA5 double-corrects) **Transferable point-wind map without a pad record.** Fit ONE rank-preserving 10 m speed map from the tool's own speeds to station speeds, conditioned on local hour, season and a stability proxy (ERA5 boundary-layer height or the tool's 10-100 m shear), pooled over several airports of the region (TCS, MAF, plus a few more NM/TX ASOS from IEM, small download) and USCRN. Test leave-one-station-out: does a map fitted on the others improve KS / calm share / CRPS at the left-out station? If yes it can ship default-off as `Launch.calibrate_site('regional')` for pads without their own anemometer record. Why it should work where W4 could not: it uses the information (station speeds) that ERA5 lacks, and the leave-one-out test answers whether it transfers. CPU, about 1.5 h. State: sideline.

- IL-166 (from V6, 2026-11-09) **Balloon-coverage widening of mid-troposphere temperature.** Density at 5-7 km is unbiased against balloons but its 90 % interval holds the balloon only 79-83 % of the time (86-90 % below 3 km); the scorecard against ERA5 has its lowest cov99 in T 5-12 km too. ERA5-fitted spread calibration cannot see the balloon-minus-ERA5 scatter aloft. Test: per band, fit a spread factor on balloons 2021-23 (EPZ, MAF) for T 3-10 km, apply on 2024-25, accept if density / T coverage reach 0.88-0.92 without a CRPS loss. CPU, about 1 h. **State: DROPPED 2026-11-09 (`scripts/il166_t_spread.py`, log `docs/results/logs/il166_t_spread.log`, 148 fit / 150 test soundings):** factors fitted on 2021-23 are 0.90-1.14 (close to 1), test 2024-25 T CRPS unchanged or slightly worse at every height (e.g. 5 km 1.590 -> 1.593 K), T coverage already 0.88-0.94; the V6 density coverage of 0.79 at 5 km was partly sampling noise (0.83 on this draw; n = 150 gives about +-0.03). The temperature spread aloft is calibrated against balloons. Failure ledger: 1 of the next 3.

- 2026-11-09 failure ledger, next group: IL-166 (neutral), IL-164 (no change), C4w on Denver zero-shot (FAIL: temperature transfer worse) = 3 of 3 -> idea IL-167.
- IL-167 (learns from C4w: the near-ground wind term helps the wind in-box on two training seeds but makes the TEMPERATURE transfer worse in the unseen Denver box; the stack's Schaake shuffle imposes the joint dependence from real profiles AFTER calibration, so the joint order does not have to come from one network) **Column-wise model blend before the shuffle.** Take the u, v columns from the C4w model and T, RH and the surface columns from the base model (each with its own range calibration), then shuffle with the same template and apply the shear and lapse maps: the wind gain of C4w without its temperature cost out of the box. Test in-box (l21, 3 score seeds) and Denver zero-shot, paired against both parents; check the physics rows (shear and lapse are re-imposed by the maps). CPU + scoring, about 1 h. **State: DONE 2026-11-09 17:10 (`score --blend-wind M --blend-calib C`, opt-in; `scripts/gpu_direct_il167.sh`): in-box (l21, DAY seeds 0 / 1 / 2, NIGHT) the blend beats base + lapse map by +0.0012 / +0.0013 / +0.0012 / +0.0005 (0-1 km u +0.0078 / +0.0045 / ... ) but stays below the full C4w stack (-0.0005 / -0.0008 / -0.0006 / -0.0014); Denver zero-shot it equals the base exactly (+0.0100 vs +0.0100; T 1-5 km +0.0083 and t2m +0.0086 against the C4w version): it removes C4w's out-of-box temperature cost but keeps only part of the in-box gain. Not adopted for the tool (NM/TX and Denver have separate models, full C4w is best in-box); kept as the recipe for ONE model over several regions (global plan).**

- 2026-11-09 failure ledger after IL-167: P1 difference assimilation (neutral: equals the raw update, the tool knows neither site's siting) = 1 of the next 3. P2 gust as a mixed-layer measurement (-2.3 % in mixed air, ~0 at night: too small) = 2 of the next 3. P4 regime-dependent spread (FAIL: the factor is a property of the site, not of the regime) = 3 of 3 -> idea IL-168.
- IL-168 (learns from P1: the tool's prediction is the same at station and pad, so differences cannot remove siting bias; P4: the needed spread factor is a property of the SITE, not of day / night or boundary-layer depth; GW-F / M5c: airports need x1.2 and an upward scale, the basin needs neither; M6: one month of the site's own data fixes it) **Site-representativeness model from static site descriptors.** Predict a site's wind scale factor and spread factor from what is known everywhere without a record: the directional roughness z0 (`tempest_weather_v2/roughness.py`, NLCD), the terrain position (TPI at 1-5 km, valley / basin / ridge), the distance to the nearest ridge and the elevation difference to the ERA5 grid cell. Fit it across every station with a record (9 ASOS airports, USCRN Las Cruces, USCRN Monahans; more USCRN and mesonet sites are a small download) as factor = f(descriptors), test leave-one-site-out. If it transfers, every pad without its own anemometer gets a site calibration (the -21 to -25 % of M6) from its descriptors alone. CPU, about 2 h plus a small land-cover download. State: sideline, promoted to the plan as P7. **RESULT 2026-11-09: NEUTRAL** (terrain adds about 0.5 % over one constant regional factor, 8 sites; see plan P7). Failure ledger: 1 of the next 3.
- IL-169 (2026-11-10, learns from G3b: per-point templates = 2-degree templates (co-extreme 0.053 both, ERA5 0.088), no shuffle 0.022): the shuffle carries the co-extremes but loses about 40 % of them. Diagnose on CPU between GPU scores: (1) the co-extreme ratio of the template pool itself (if about 0.088 the templates are fine), (2) the shuffled model with its marginals swapped for the templates' (marginal effect), (3) shuffling in (10 m wind vector, 0-1 km shear vector) coordinates instead of raw u / v per height. If (3) restores it, a flag in the shuffle (`--shuffle-space shear`), off by default, scored paired. Failure-rule count: 2 of 3 (P7 neutral, G3b per-point templates neutral).
- IL-170 / D-1 (2026-11-10, learns from DENEXP + L21b): the new Denver tool model has no post-processing; on NM/TX the lapse map (superadiabatic 33.8 -> 23.6 %, T better in every band), the shear map and the 2-degree templates each helped. Fit the three for l3_blocal_c4w on denver_full_dpd (fit 2021-23), score 2024-25 paired against the plain model, export the ones that pass (export approved for this model; the post-processing is the same recipe as the NM/TX bundle). Also: why was generator_denver.pt below climatology (-0.115)? Check if it was ever scored on this dataset (normalisation / store mismatch) so the same failure cannot hide in a future region export (IL-171: an export gate that scores the exported tool on held-out rows before it is installed).
- IL-169 RESULT (2026-11-10 00:45): neutral for a fix (shear-space shuffle 0.049-0.052 vs 0.052-0.053), but it corrects the premise: against the stable ERA5 reference (climatology ensemble 0.068-0.073) the tool is about 25 % low, not half; the truth-row reference is noisy (7-22 joint events). Failure-rule count 3 of 3 (P7, G3b per-point templates, IL-169 shear space) -> new idea IL-172; count reset to 0.
- IL-172 (2026-11-10, failure-rule idea, learns from IL-169: the shuffle hands on about 78 % of the co-extremes its templates hold, and ERA5 templates hold 0.07 where balloons show 0.17): BALLOON templates for the 0-3 km wind columns. Station-corrected IGRA soundings (EPZ 72364, MAF 72265; training years <= 2023) interpolated to the model heights give the rank template of u / v at 0-3 km, ERA5 templates stay for everything else (rank blend at the 3 km seam). Test: co-extreme ratio and wind CRPS / coverage against held-out 2024-25 balloons (not ERA5, which shares the deficit, B30), paired, two score seeds; flag off by default. Risk: 00 / 12 UTC only (no daytime-hour template), about 700 soundings per month-bin pooled over the two sites.

**What worked (numbers):**

The 00Z + 12Z forecast refetch (on disk since Nov 6) showed the shipped mode F already has skill 0.67-0.74 at 05 / 11 h; window widened 14-20 -> 02-20 h. Denver mode F (first one) trained on the Denver refetch: skill 0.68 / 0.57 / 0.43 / 0.26 at 1 / 3 / 5 / 7 d.

ERA5 cannot referee between versions of itself (B30); balloons can, at both 00Z and 12Z.

**The pattern:** the pre-registered replicate did its job: a near-pass on one hour set failed on a second set at a different station, so the near-pass was noise at the floor, not a hidden pass. TCS is the binding constraint everywhere: its own spread (no report 0.83-0.84, own report 0.82) caps what any neighbour fix can reach.
**New ideas in the working direction:**
- IL-207: fix the TCS spread first (the target, not observe): a per-site 10 m spread check at TCS vs MAF (coverage 0.84 vs 0.94 with no report) - is TCS a terrain site the generator under-spreads? Then re-score A3-fix B against that corrected base.
- IL-208: A3-fix B2 = B with rho = gain x (1 - representativeness of dz) using the REP_DZ term already in obs.py, since ONM (the B failure in run 2) is the nearest but sits across the Rio Grande valley; measure on the same two hour seeds (no new fit).
- IL-209: extend the trained area check: 3 of 7 neighbours (ONM, ABQ, FST) are outside it; any neighbour-based method (D, sodar referee, 18-80 km item) needs the prior there, so list which Part 5 items are blocked by this before building them.
